ITALIAN CENTER FOR UFOLOGICAL STUDIES




              USOCAT
                 ITALIAN CATALOG OF
                  OBJECT SIGHTINGS
               UNIDENTIFIED SUBMERGED




               by MARCO BIANCHINI




1
                                    USOCAT PROJECT

The UsoCat project, born in March 1995, on the initiative of the curator Marco Bianchini (member
of CISU - Italian Center for Ufological Studies) is aimed at collecting all reports relating to
observations of USO (unidentified submerged objects) in seas, rivers and lakes Italians.


                                       CLASSIFICATIONS


For the classification of reports, the following four categories were adopted:
A - Objects and lights completely submerged throughout the sighting (those that are considered
actual USE).
B - Objects that enter the water, whether they fall (which also fall under the CrashCat), or whether
they dive after having performed some evolutions, or whether they dive and then re-emerge to
disappear into the sky (for classification purposes, the way with which the first contact with the
water element occurs).
C - Objects that come out of the water (it doesn't matter if they later re-enter it).
D - Objects sailing or stationary on the surface of the water.


APPENDIX A – This appendix contains all reports regarding sightings of ghost submarines and
ghost ships, as well as cases regarding strange marine accidents and discoveries of military
material in the water.
These are borderline cases, which fall more properly into FortCat, but which are equally taken into
consideration by the editor of UsoCat, even if they are then included in the catalog in a separate
appendix.
Generally, cases of ghost submarines or ghost ships are cataloged separately only when the sources
never mention UFOs, but in particular submarines of unknown nationalities. In the appendix
concerning strange accidents and ghost submarines, all the discoveries of military material which
have occurred in water (acoustic buoys, projectiles, torpedoes) are also catalogued, again only
when UFOs are never specifically mentioned in the sources, otherwise they are more properly in
the UsoCat (see more in-depth explanation below).
APPENDIX B - In addition to these borderline cases, the information that is part of the so-called

2
"usological folklore" is also collected, i.e. those stories and legends concerning alleged alien bases
under the surface of Italian seas and lakes, also included in the separate appendix ( see the stories
that tell of alien bases at the bottom of Lake Trasimeno in Umbria).
All the information regarding cases included in these two appendices is collected and cataloged for
the pure spirit of completeness. Obviously from a ufological point of view the news, listed in
Appendix B, has no scientific value; they are traditionally part of the subject and are that mixture of
"folklore and mythology" that every branch of knowledge must possess.


APPENDIX C – In the last appendix it seemed useful and interesting to collect all the information
relating to curiosities related to the sea and UFOs: for example, an American patent for a USO is
catalogued, or strange reports of underground seas and oceans. It is essentially a short appendix in
which all the curious and unreliable information is collected, which however can be potentially
interesting for the complete study of the USO phenomenon. This appendix also includes reports of
USO sightings which occurred outside from Italian territorial waters but which have ships flying
the Italian flag as protagonists.
Some news has no direct relevance to the phenomenon of UFOs and USOs, but concerns strange
marine phenomena that have occurred in Italian territorial waters and therefore it was deemed
useful to catalog them anyway (see for example the phenomenon of the Panarea whirlpools at the
end of 2002).


                                             SOURCES


All known sources relating to each case have been included in the catalogue, even if they are not in
the curator's possession.
Generally, most of the sources relating to USO sightings come from the press and, with very rare
and sporadic exceptions, there are no direct investigations by investigators. It is often the case that
second-hand sources have erroneously indicated some details such as the date of the sighting, the
witnesses, the objects observed and occasionally even the places: some examples exist in the 1970s,
especially in the famous Adriatic wave of 1978 By carefully reading the various newspaper articles
of the time, it is possible to notice similarities between the individual episodes narrated.
As regards book sources, it is curious to note that there are no specialized texts on the USO. Even

3
the historical "InvisibleResident" by Ivan T. Sanderson and some texts by Keel, which however do
not catalog the sightings or enumerate classification methodologies, tend to propose only theories
of life under the sea. In Italy no text on the subject has ever been published. The various books that
deal with ufology, in general, always have a chapter on the USO, but they limit themselves to
including the usual famous foreign cases (perhaps with the classic connection to the Bermuda
Triangle), the Gorgona case of 06/22/1979 and some mention of the Adriatic wave of
autumn-winter 1978. There are no references to serious or scientific studies, but these texts only try
to attract the public's attention by mixing the mystery of UFOs with that of the sea. At the end of
2003, a text by Roberto Pinotti entitled “Unidentified Submerged Objects” was published by the
Olimpia editions of Florence; unfortunately, as is the author's habit, the text is only a collage of his
previous books, with entire chapters on the Bermuda Triangle, on alien bases under the sea and
little or nothing on case studies. The Italian cases cataloged by Pinotti do not even reach 30.
Unfortunately, therefore, the cataloging work was not simple and above all it is not complete due to
the absolute lack of serious sources and accurate investigations.


                                        CLARIFICATIONS


To classify a given event, its initial behavior is taken into consideration. For example, when a
witness sees a light coming out of the water and then after several evolutions re-enters it, the case
will be labeled "C". However, if the witness sees underwater lights moving for a certain time and
eventually coming out of the sea, the case will be classified as "A".
For easily understandable reasons, only territorial waters are taken into consideration (according to
international law - 12 nautical miles), and therefore the curator will be able to evaluate, at his
discretion, whether or not to include a given sighting. This is caused by the fact that in many reports
the witness indicated a stretch of sea, but was unable to establish the distance from the coast,
without precise reference points. However, indicatively, the Tyrrhenian, Ionian, Ligurian and
Adriatic seas in their entirety are considered territorial waters, for the sole purpose of including a
case in the UsoCat, and as far as the Mediterranean is concerned, only that which borders the Italian
coasts. No problem arises for lakes and rivers, as they are internal waters.
As regards the individual summaries of the cases, it is necessary to point out that these are often
very short because the sources in the editor's possession are truly scarce; very often these are

4
journalistic blurbs of very few lines and nothing more. However, everything reported in the
description of the sighting is what emerges from the sources.


                                            EXCLUSIONS


All reports of objects and lights prior to January 1, 1900 are excluded from the UsoCat.
This is due to the fact that cases prior to 1900 are considered to be the responsibility of PreUfoCat
and since, in the opinion of the editor of the catalogue, these must be analyzed and investigated
with investigative methodologies different from those of modern ufology. In any case, just out of
curiosity, only one of the cases prior to 1900 has been found and most likely it didn't even happen
in Italian territorial waters.
As regards category "B" (that of objects entering the water), falls from the sky of objects such as
animals, blood, sand, etc. are excluded. which fall more properly into FortCat. In fact, it is believed
that cases of artificial objects or of meteoric origin must be included. The falls of ice fragments are
included as the CrashCat also catalogs such episodes and therefore it was deemed appropriate not
to conflict with another CISU catalogue, in which there is a specific category which concerns the
UsoCat (category "C ” CrashCat which collects cases of objects fallen into the sea).
Cases in which only columns of water, wave motions or underwater bubbles were observed,
without the sighting of lights or objects, are not included in the catalogue.
Also excluded from the catalog are stories regarding sightings and legends of sea monsters, snakes
and so on, since these episodes fall within the scope of cryptozoology and therefore in FortCat.
However, as regards the phenomenon of incandescent stones, often identified as pieces of
phosphorus bombs, it is necessary to specify that these are cataloged only when they are seen
falling into the sea or are recovered in the water. In the latter case, the sighting input must not be the
observation of the actual stone, but of the light emanating from it or any movements of the object.
Radar-only phenomena without visual contact with the object are also excluded.




                                      ADRIATIC WAVE 1978


As regards the wave of sightings that affected the Adriatic coast in the autumn-winter of 1978, only

5
the cases relating to observations of lights or objects have been included; in relation to the
observations of water columns, seismic, gaseous and radar phenomena, these were not taken into
consideration, since they do not fall within the actual UsoCat and since there is a specific study
project relating to this famous wave of the Adriatic Sea.
Certainly the Adriatic phenomenon is undoubtedly one of the most interesting in the entire Italian
UFO panorama and the hope is that it can be analyzed in great detail and with scientific
investigation methodologies, something which unfortunately has never been done at the time of
facts. The writer's belief is that the majority of sightings of the Adriatic Flap are not the result of
actually observed phenomena or strange underwater anomalies (gas bubbles, methane, earthquakes)
as claimed by many, but rather originate from tensions and relationships which, in that period, had
been created between fishermen and the coastal authorities.


                              POSSIBLE IDENTIFYING CAUSES


The editor deemed it useful to give each case a specific personal evaluation label which is divided
as follows:
1 - Insufficient information : the cases distinguished in this way do not have sufficient
information to proceed with any scientific analysis; most sightings are marked with this label
because the information in our possession is few or second-hand, often only journalistic and there
have been no direct and accurate investigations. The few investigations present are the result of the
ufological culture of the seventies. Meager reporting forms, mostly filled out by very young people,
whose only interest was to increase sightings, without trying to delve into any aspect in depth. A
sighting can, in the opinion of the curator, be declared unidentified only when all other possible or
probable identifying causes have been excluded and only after a thorough investigation. It is
inconceivable for modern ufology to declare an object or light unidentified, about which there is no
precise information and on which no investigation has been carried out with a scientific
investigative methodology. Finally, it is useful to remember that most of the objects seen falling
into the sea have never been identified or recovered.
2 - Possible explanation : this label has marked the cases in which it is possible to evaluate some
hypotheses, which can reasonably explain the sighting.
3 - Probable explanation : in this case there is a concrete probability, given the methods of

6
sighting and an analogical reconstruction of the event, that the phenomenon can be traced back to a
specific and probable explanation.
4 – ISO (identified submerged object) : the phenomenon has been identified with the utmost
certainty and there are no doubts about its nature.
5 – USE (unidentified submerged object) : in this case we are in the presence of sufficient
information regarding the events that took place, the witnesses, the place, the weather conditions,
etc. and there does not seem to be any possible or probable explanation of the phenomenon in the
current state of knowledge. Only very few cases are so marked and this happens when there is a lot
of news or there have been serious investigations carried out according to a scientific investigation
methodology.


In general, it was possible to notice how some events can be traced back to an explanation as
follows:
A - The cases concerning this category can generally be explained by natural phenomena such as
cetaceans, fish or artificial phenomena such as submarines, torpedoes, towed sonars, ROVs, sailing
just under the surface of the water, or by the activity of divers .
B - The objects that enter the water can be of all types, but the most typical examples, with which
some reports are explained, are meteoric phenomena, fragments of satellites, sounding balloons or
parts of them, parachutes, airplanes, etc.
C - The third category is the one that creates the most problems, since there are only two objects
that can come out of the water: missiles launched by submerged units and signal rockets; the latter
do not come out of the sea, but are launched from its surface, even if in the distance the optical
effect they can generate in an observer is that they have come out of the water. Other objects that
come out of the sea can only be cetaceans that jump and then return, but in the catalog there is no
case that can be traced back to this hypothesis, which is otherwise very extreme and remote.
D - The objects in the fourth category can be traced back to observations of cetaceans or large fish
swimming on the surface, like whales when they breathe, and from boats of any kind or rocks or
any other drifting object (logs, algae, plastic, etc. ) which are not recognized by witnesses.


As regards the category of objects that enter the water, it must be said that the editor of the catalog
would have ventured some plausible or at least possible hypotheses, but he often limited himself to

7
classifying them as "insufficient information", since with this classification they had been included
in the CrashCat; it was deemed appropriate to conform to the indication in the CrashCat catalogue,
precisely to avoid future inconsistencies and incompatibilities between the two study projects.


                             PLANES DRILLING INTO THE SEA


A curious phenomenon that is numerically very interesting is that of false alarms relating to planes
that were seen crashing into the sea. Generally these stories have the same common thread, namely
the presence of a small tourist plane, clearly in difficulty, with a trail of smoke coming out of the
back and which after a dive is seen diving into the sea.
This is a curious phenomenon since it is not concentrated in a particular period of time, but includes
the entire UsoCat in its entirety, even if the 1990s are particularly prolific in these reports. Every
time there are search and rescue operations carried out by air and naval means and coordinated by
the competent Port Authorities. However, there is not a single case in which these research
operations have given positive results.
Personally I believe that the majority of similar reports can be explained by errors of perception or
false alarms and in any case the entire phenomenology must, in the editor's opinion, be included in
a sociological aspect and be studied as such.


                                   FUTURE PERSPECTIVES


It is not easy to indicate what the future prospects of UsoCat may be. There would be many,
starting with retrospective investigations of individual cases, especially those relating to the 1978
wave, about which so much has been written in the newspapers, but on which at the same time
there are no serious and accurate inquiries or investigations. The fishermen, the main witnesses of
USO sightings, are not easy people to approach and the chances of being able to meet and interview
them are slim.
A good starting point, but for the sole purpose of researching sources and expanding the number of
reports, is to carry out research in library archives on old period newspapers, which often carry
articles and short articles that are interesting more from a historical and of cataloguing, rather than
from a scientific point of view.

8
Despite the great interest that the USO and any mystery related to the sea arouses in people and
ufologists, the percentage of USO sightings compared to UFOs is very low.
Excluding the 1978 wave, with a normal relapse in 1979, reports from subsequent years are truly
rare, especially in recent years, with an average of around 2 cases per year. Certainly this does not
facilitate a phenomenon, which interests and fascinates everyone in a superficial way, but which
has never been studied in depth.
The objective of the curator of this catalog is to continuously and annually update the sightings and
above all the sources of the individual episodes; draw up a complete catalog with detailed and
precise summaries of each report; contact colleagues in other countries who manage similar USO
catalogues, to increase exchanges of material and above all of research methodologies; finally, the
most important future perspective is to directly investigate at least the most sensational and
potentially interesting episodes, to try to explain what happens under the waters of the Italian seas
and lakes.


                                   FINAL CONSIDERATIONS


The phenomenon of USOs is very particular, since it occurs in an extremely different element from
that of UFOs and requires specific investigative procedures for researchers to be identified, also in
consideration of the fact that most of the witnesses are usually professional fishermen, who spend
half of their life at sea and who are therefore experts on everything that happens in the hydrosphere
and around them.
It does not appear that there have ever been serious researchers specialized on the subject (apart
from the American Ivan T. Sanderson - mostly for the original and curious theories, rather than for
serious scientific research) and therefore also the normal procedures of investigation have never
been directly specified and described. Suffice it to say, unlike what happens with sightings of
objects in the air, that in the sea there is the element of water which in itself requires in-depth
analysis. An object entering or leaving the sea or sailing on or under its surface creates a wave or
splashes of water, which are objective and visible data. If a vessel is nearby it is affected by the
wave and roll. When a report comes from a ship, we must remember that it always has a radar,
often a sonar, sophisticated instruments such as GPS, echo sounders and therefore the possibilities
of scientific research increase exponentially; coordinates, longitude, latitude, radar tracks, depth,

9
sea strength, salinity and so on are often available to researchers. Precisely because of this
environment, for the particularity and sometimes the experience of the witnesses (for example,
there are millions of people on earth, but in principle the sea is navigated and lived by those who
know it), for the lack of points of reference, for the equipment on board ships, it is appropriate and
necessary to equip oneself with a "usological" investigation methodology which must necessarily
be different from the classic and well-tested one, which is used in

              by a newspaper that it had simply been the usual April Fool's prank.

Description   On the night between the 24th and 25th, Mr. Carlo Rossi, aged 53, resident in San
of sighting   Pietro a Vico, witnessed a disconcerting event. Rossi, being a passionate fisherman,
              went fishing that night in the Serchio, a river not far from San Pietro a Vico.
              Walking along a path in the dark along the river, he was attracted by an unusual
              phenomenon; from one point of the river an unnatural light spread. Intrigued by the
              fact, Rossi approached the point from which the light came, remaining in the shelter
              of the embankment; thinking he was hallucinating, he saw suspended over the river
              and at a low altitude, a strange circular vehicle that seemed to be supplied with
              water through a long tube that was drawn from the river. The enormous object was
              hovering silently in the air with a system of propellers. Rossi judged that the object
              had a diameter of about 25 meters. It resembled a round disk with a thickness of two
              meters and was painted black. All around, on the thickness, there were oval
              openings like mouths. In the center was a turret that projected three meters below
              the disk and half a meter above. It was transparent as if made of glass or plastic. The
              turret was as large as a room and must have had a diameter of five meters; inside
              you could see four thin tubes connected with a large cylinder in the middle. A bluish
              flame, with orange flashes similar to a blowtorch, passed from one cylinder to
              another, alternately rising and falling. The glow came from the transparent turret
              and therefore reflected on the surface of the disk, making it luminous. That strange
              device had five propellers underneath, halfway out of the black circle, all arranged
              around it, equidistant from each other. In the upper part, however, there were three
              propellers: one as large as the entire circumference of the disk, the other two on an
              increasingly smaller scale. The object emitted a slight buzz, like a "silk rustle".
              Suddenly a porthole opened in the upper part of the turret and a human figure
              looked out and peered around, who probably identified the witness, since he stuck
              out an arm as if to point it out to someone who was inside the object. It was at this
              point that Rossi, shaken by the torpor into which he had fallen at the vision, fled by
              throwing himself headlong down the embankment slope. As soon as he reached the
              bottom, he saw above his head a green ray suddenly cut through the air in his
              direction, feeling at that moment a shock throughout his body, as if he were hit by
              an electric shock . Terrified, he threw himself to the ground. Raising his head, he
              saw the disc rise over the embankment and rapidly gain altitude, disappearing at
              frightening speed in the direction of Viareggio.
              Given the historical importance of the case, we transcribe in full the article
              published by La Nazione Italiana on 09/26/1952 by Beppe Pegolotti ( 1906-1990),
              a Florentine journalist known for decades in Tuscany for his reporting sports.
              Here is the text, almost in its entirety:
              “Lucca, 25. – The other day, a man showed up at the Public Prosecutor's Office to
17
     talk about flying saucers. The speech was linked to a strange incident which,
     according to him, would have put his life in danger. Since flying saucers, and
     everything related to them, do not have, at least for now, a legal personality or a
     well-defined personality, no denunciation has been put on paper. The man,
     logically, was only told to report other episodes, perhaps more "concrete", that
     could occur. We were able to approach that man... and have him repeat the whole
     story. In fact, we went with him to the scene of the "bad crime". The scene is a
     stretch of the Serchio, facing the town of San Pietro a Vico, four and a half
     kilometers from the center of Lucca.
     The night of July 24th. Here: we are on the river, near the high embankment that
     protects the wonderful countryside cultivated with vegetables and orchards. The
     closest house is that of Bandoni, known as Geppetto, and is located three or four
     hundred meters away, on the road called "del Brennero". The place is actually
     called Piana dei Tacchini. Over there, beyond the river, the Apuan mountains
     dominate, you can see Ponte a Moriano with the entrance to the Garfagnana and,
     above, the Convent of the Angel, of the Passionist friars. The watercourse, near a
     winding bend, is forty meters wide and deep. That's enough. Let's give the narrator
     the floor and listen to him without comments. The man says: “ It was the night of
     July 24th or 25th, I don't remember well. I am a passionate fisherman, I used to
     come here with the scales, like many other times. What time? It must have been
     three o'clock. There was no moon. I was coming from that path, from Geppetto's
     house and, having arrived at the embankment, I saw a light. What happened? I
     remained under the embankment and headed towards the point where the light
     came from. So, I arrived at this very point and then I climbed up to see who was
     there, at that very unusual hour, shining a light on the river. I was down here, so I
     stuck my head out...". The man repeats the scene seriously. He leans over the
     embankment and continues: “There was a flying saucer. As? It really was there. No,
     not sitting on the water! The propellers were in motion and the disc was, like a
     helicopter, one meter from the surface. I'm not joking, I know what I'm saying. I
     remained at least ten minutes watching him, with great attention. The flying saucer
     supplied itself with water, because a hose hung from the lower end of the turret and
     dipped into the river. I had the opportunity to observe many details, I could draw it
     perfectly and I can tell you without a doubt that certain photographs that appeared
     in some newspapers, photographs of disks that appeared in the sky of Sarzana for
     example, do not give any idea of what it is about. You will understand: I saw him, at
     my convenience, at a distance of fifteen metres. Look, judge the distance for
     yourself: the disc was there!... ". A record, then. Again the narrator speaks: “How
     big was he, how was he made? Here you are. It had a diameter that I judged to be
     twenty-five meters. A record, really round, without bells and whistles. Its thickness
     was two meters. Painted in black. All around, on the thickness, there were oval
     openings, like mouths. I would say that from those the disc sucked air which was
     probably used to drive the engine. In the center there was a turret. It protruded three
     meters below the disc and half a meter above. It was transparent, as if made of glass
     or plastic. This turret must have had a diameter of five meters, it was as big, in short,
     as a room. Inside you could see four thin cylinders connected with a large cylinder
     in the middle. A bluish flame, with glimmers of orange, similar to a blowtorch,
     passed from one cylinder to another, alternately rising and falling. The glow came
18
     from the transparent turret and was therefore reflected on the lower surface of the
     disk, making it luminous. That flying saucer had five propellers underneath:
     propellers that came out halfway from the black circle, arranged round and round,
     equidistant from each other. In the upper part, however, higher than the top of the
     turret, there were three propellers: one, the lowest, as large as the entire
     circumference of the disk, the other two, in scale, increasingly smaller...". Minute,
     very detailed description , no doubt about it, but the best is yet to come. Let's hear:
     Danger escaped. “I was here watching and I was no longer able to move. The disc
     was also motionless, suspended between air and water. The engine made no noise.
     Just a subdued buzz, like a rustle of silk. And you had to listen to it. Suddenly,
     however, something new happened. A porthole opened at the top of the turret which
     is not transparent. And from that porthole a man appeared, in full light. Yes, a man.
     How was it made? He had a diving suit. He spent a few moments peering around
     then suddenly made a move as if to point at me. He stuck out an arm. He had seen
     me. Maybe I had raised my head too high on the bank. I threw myself down the
     embankment and, almost at the same time, I saw a green ray pierce the night, above
     the embankment, in my direction. I felt a shock throughout my body and, looking
     up, I saw the flying saucer disappearing. Yes, it disappeared with the speed of
     lightning. It was like following the path of a tracer bullet. In a fraction of a second it
     disappeared high up, towards Viareggio!” . Therefore, not the slightest trace
     remained of the presence of the flying saucer. If only the water supply pipe had
     remained! Nope... The man must have fled away with almost the same speed as the
     disc, such was his fear. He says he entered the house with his heart in his throat. He
     says he didn't even say a word to his wife. And he would never have opened his
     mouth, he added, if another sensational event hadn't happened to him about ten days
     ago. It went like this: Chapter two “In broad daylight, at four in the afternoon, I
     come to fish in this same place and I find another fisherman there. He had a short
     cane, wore a blue overall, was very thin, tall, with a tapered nose and gray eyes. He
     immediately looks me up and down, then comes and sits next to me as I scale the
     scales. Start chatting. “How did he speak? In Italian, but with a foreign accent.
     American accent? No. We Lucca people recognize it well. I would not know. I
     thought he was a Nordic. So, he sits down and talks about fish. Then, out of the
     blue, he asks me if I have ever seen airplanes or things that fly on the river. I hasten
     to say no. Then he goes quiet, stares at me with gray eyes, offers me a cigarette. I
     take it mechanically. It is a long, thin cigarette, with the brand in gold. It lights it up
     for me. I take two puffs and immediately feel like I'm dying. My head is spinning,
     my vision is blurring. And, mind you, I'm an avid smoker !... I immediately put out
     the cigarette, I pretend to put it in my pocket, but the man in the suit grabs me by the
     wrist, snatches the cigarette from my hand, breaks it into pieces, throws it into the
     river...". Terrible. And then? And then the stranger ran away and our man, as soon
     as he recovered, went home stunned. He wanted to vent. He couldn't take it
     anymore. He told the story to his wife who at first looked at him astonished and then
     sent him to tell it to the chaplain of the church of San Pietro a Vico, Don Giuseppe.
     The man was convinced he felt in danger, he connected the two, he saw the stranger
     as an attacker. Why did he want to hurt him? Eh, because that foreigner had to be "a
     friend of the flying saucer". He therefore escaped the discharge of the "death ray",
     he escaped the poisoning of the "witness-suppressing cigarette".
19
              The man who told these things lives in San Pietro a Vico and, with his wife, his son,
              his daughter-in-law and a grandson. He is fifty-three years old, having been born in
              Saltocchio, near Ponte a Moriano, in 1899. He lacks his left arm, which was
              removed in a railway investment in 1926, and works with agricultural machinery in
              the Lucca countryside. His first name is Carlo, we don't say his surname because we
              had to promise him not to publish it. We asked about him on the spot. Everyone,
              even Don Giuseppe, spoke to us of him as a person considered normal. The story
              cannot be explained. “ A serious person – added Don Giuseppe – perhaps a little
              cheerful, with a hint of jokingness”. Pranksterry."
              Pegolotti's article was accompanied by a striking and accurate drawing, the author
              of which is not cited, but who was presumably a collaborator of the Florentine
              newspaper. It shows a disc of reduced thickness, smooth, with four elongated
              "portholes" placed along the edge. Three small tripal helices protrude from the
              lower part, placed parallel to the water, and, in the center of the disk itself, always
              below, a sort of large and long cylinder marked by some stripes. At the bottom,
              under the cylinder, what presumably had to be the "tube" immersed vertically in the
              waters of the Serchio. As for the upper part of the disk, in the centre, corresponding
              to the lower "cylinder", its "extension" was visible, i.e. a cabin with a hatch,
              presumably with a transparent window, wide open to the left and, inside, the face
              and shoulders of a man who appears to be wearing a pilot's helmet on his head.
              Finally, above this "cabin", there is an even more peculiar element: a long vertical
              beam along which three two-bladed propellers of progressively smaller dimensions
              are placed.

                                                                                 Roberts, 1985; Life
                                                                                 Science      March

Description   Several people were able to observe for a few minutes two objects evolving in the
of sighting   sky, almost touching the surface of the sea. A newspaper described them as
              platforms surmounted by a central turret. On the sea these two objects sent
              reflections of whitish light and for a few minutes they traveled a few kilometers on
              the surface of the water in perfect synchronism. After a while, they did a wheelie
              almost touching the balconies of some hotels in Taormina. Subsequently, one of
              the two objects reared up again and disappeared behind the mountains, while the
              second object fell into the sea, sinking about three hundred meters from the beach.
              The phenomenon was observed by many tourists including a journalist from the
              New York Herald Tribune who took some photos, which were then published by
              the American newspaper. Two Carabinieri, with their boat, then headed to the site
              of the impact and recovered a large, floppy nylon casing weighing approximately
              100 kilograms. This last circumstance, as well as that of the photo, were denied by
              a careful retrospective investigation by CISU members Antonio Blanco, Salvatore
              Foresta and Antonio Rampulla, according to which the casing was recovered not
              by the Carabinieri, but rather by the fishermen Cosimo and Antonio
              Carnabuci, witnesses of the facts, who delivered the object to the local Guardia di
              Finanza station. The investigators then ascertained that the author of the famous
              image, which went around the world through the press , was not - as believed until
              then by some - an American journalist from the "New York Herald Tribune", but
              the photographer Giuseppe Grasso and that the photo - taken from the seafront of
              Giardini-Naxos and not from the Belvedere balcony of Taormina, as everyone had
              always believed - was artfully retouched to give greater prominence to the "
              records". Not only that: in-depth research carried out in the newspaper libraries
              local authorities, also allowed the discovery, in the local press of the time, of
              in-depth journalistic reports on the case, containing other images of the event,
              including a second photo of the "discs" which was practically unknown and
              another depicting the floppy casing of one of the recovered weather balloons ,
              despite those who, for almost 35 years, have continued to pass off their image as
              that of real flying saucers.
              This case demonstrates how an accurate retrospective investigation, based on a
              scientific investigation methodology, can identify and clarify many aspects of a
              case considered by the press and by a certain ufology to be "mysterious".

Description   At approximately 10.30 pm, from the veranda of a restaurant, the waiters and
of sighting   some customers saw in the sea, not far from the shore, numerous intense
              phosphorescent lights, blue in color and circular in shape, which were performing
              strange evolutions. Seven witnesses decided at that point to take a boat and head
              to the point where these lights could be seen, also because it was not possible to
              identify the phenomenon from the beach. Having boarded the "Santa Maria" boat,
              they approached the lights, but they moved away and sank shortly after. Only one
              of these remained afloat on the surface of the sea, interrupting its evolution. It
              appeared to be a metallic body shaped like a dolphin, with three antennas about a
              meter high protruding from the front, similar to a dolphin head. At that point one
              of the witnesses, Mr. Ettore Java, hit the object with an oar and all the occupants
              of the boat were hit by an electric shock. Immediately afterwards the object
              disappeared, plunging into the sea. After the septa, recovered from the shock,
              moved away, they were able to see that the lights had reappeared on the surface of
              the sea and that they were moving away towards the horizon, leaving behind a
              streak of flaming light. The hypotheses of the time suggested by the newspapers
              were that it was some military device used in naval exercises. Without a doubt it is
              one of the most interesting cases of the entire Italian USO panorama, since in
              addition to the observation of a luminous phenomenon, there was also a close

Description   Some people claimed to have seen an object shaped like a parachute fall into the
of sighting   sea. A photographer, Willy Colombini, managed to take some photos.
              Unfortunately, these were not published, but a newspaper faithfully reports the
              description of what remained imprinted on them: an object was clearly visible
              above a huge swimmer. They were three black dots on a single vertical. The most
              central one was larger and with a hemispherical shape; the two smaller ones
              seemed to be tied by a thread. Numerous boats, including the motor ship
              Mergellina, carried out a search at sea, which however did not yield any positive
              results. From the detailed description of the photographs, which were developed
              in the presence of the Carabinieri and a marshal of the Italian Air Force, it is
              probable that the object seen falling was a parachute, perhaps one of those used for
              some meteorological experiments.
              Update: the photos taken by Colombini were later found and delivered by his
              nephew Armando Bellelli, who published them on Facebook; in these you can see
              three spherical objects, one below the other, which seem to descend towards the
              sea, supporting the hypothesis of a series of meteorological balloons.
              This is the report of the investigation carried out by CISU ufologist Salvatore
              Foresta:
              “Willy Colombini, witness and author of the photos of an object seen falling into
              the waters off the island of Capri (NA) on the afternoon of 12 December 1965.
              Reconstructing everything, Willy Colombini, Roman actor and screenwriter, was
              on the island of Capri, in particular on the terrace of the "Quisisana" hotel, to
              photograph the actress Mary La Tour, when, following the indication of her
              assistant, she noticed an object emerging from a cloud which, after having
              remained suspended in the air for two minutes, fell into the sea off the coast of the
              island. Colombini photographed the entire sequence and immediately afterwards
              notified the Port Authority which sent a ship (probably a patrol boat) and a
              seaplane to the area. The searches, however, would prove fruitless. Immediately
              afterwards Colombini took the roll of film to a development and printing shop, but
              the material was seized from him by some Air Force and Navy officers with the
              excuse of national security. The witness was only able to keep three tests for
              himself. The military also asked the witness not to tell anyone anything. The Port
              Authority would then communicate the news of the sighting to the press and in the
              following days some articles were published, without photos, in which it was said
              that the object could be a parachute or a weather balloon. Some of the newspapers
              also spoke of smuggling, given that some witnesses had declared having seen
              what appeared to be signal rockets. According to Colombini, the photographed
              object could not have been a parachute or a sounding balloon, as it seemed to him
              that it was proceeding as if "guided" and certainly not transported by winds or
              anything like that. Even falling into the sea seemed intentional to him. It seems
              strange to me that the military first advised Colombini not to talk about the matter
              with anyone, and then communicated what had happened to the press by
40
              mentioning the name and surname of the witness, with the risk that he could be
              contacted by any curious people or UFO associations. already existing at the
              time”.

              minutes, until, looking up at the sky, he no longer saw anything. After a few
              minutes the two objects reappeared and actually seemed to follow the boat. One
              was white, while the other was green and they accompanied the vessel downwind,
              about a mile away, for about an hour, after which they disappeared as suddenly as
              they had appeared.

of sighting     10 August 1977 in Francavilla a Mare (CH). From the shore of the beach some

              be classified as part of the Adriatic wave and therefore that it occurred in the
              months of October-November 1978.



     Number       Date            Location        Category         Assessment         Sources
               dd/mm/yy
      053      ???/??/1978          San               C           Insufficient    "The Journal of
                              Benedetto del                       information Mysteries" n.
                               Tronto (AP)                                        162 pages. 10.
Description   An anonymous source, who appears to be a soldier from the Port Authority of San
of sighting   Benedetto, recounts a rather singular sighting: a small boat was found intact with
63
              the lifeless body of a fisherman on board. The corpse showed very strong
              dehydration. The forensic doctors who dealt with the case indicated the time of
              death, which dated back to a couple of nights before, coinciding precisely with the
              sighting of an object from the sea at breakneck speed. It seems that both some
              sailors and the radar tracks of an unknown base or ship witnessed the sighting.
              The story continues in a science fiction way, meaning that a veil of secrecy fell on
              the matter on the part of the authorities, since that area was a stretch of sea in
              which alien spaceships tested new thrusters and often emerged and submerged. It
              therefore seems that the poor fisherman had entered an area closed to navigation.
              This case, which seems to have come from the pen of a science fiction novelist, is
              ideal for introducing us to the year 1978, a fundamental year for Italian ufology
              and for common sightings , especially those in autumn in the Adriatic. Above all
              it is emblematic because it reflects the mentality and ideas that ufologists and
              enthusiasts of the time had about the phenomenon.

     Number       Date           Location         Category        Assessment           Sources
               dd/mm/yy
      163      07/30/1978          Torre del            C          Insufficient    Testimonial
                                  Greco (NA)                       information report.
Description   Three people, who were on the terrace of their house, saw a large luminous sphere
of sighting   hovering in the air a few meters from the surface of the sea. The object appeared
              so quickly that all three witnesses had the impression that it had emerged from the
              water. The globe was red in color and the higher it rose in altitude, the more its
              brightness increased, causing white streaks to appear, which were present only on
              one side. The object, approximately 1.5 km away, appeared to have an apparent
              size of 35-40 centimeters in diameter and was located at an angular height of
              approximately 45°. Suddenly it had a very strong acceleration and disappeared
              towards the hinterland. This is an "edge" case since the object was never seen in
              contact with water. The fact that it was also included in the UsoCat is only
              because all three witnesses had the clear sensation that it had emerged from the
              sea, even if they did not directly see this emergence.

Description   The fishermen Fausto Ricci and Florindo Soncini, on board the fishing vessel
of sighting   Rapepì (or according to other sources Repepè), saw for about thirty seconds, on
              the surface of the sea, which was calm, a large dark body similar to the hump of a
              whale. The shape, which was larger than the boat, i.e. over twenty meters in
              length, was about two hundred meters away from the stern and, after thirty
              seconds, immersed without any noise in a stretch of sea no more than twelve
              meters deep. The press reports do not agree with the investigation carried out by
              Morricone according to which the witness Ricci, during the entire sighting, was in
              the cabin, in the area there was mist with visibility of about two kilometres;
              furthermore, the fishermen, at that moment, were fishing and had their nets in the
              water and above all Ricci would have seen a gigantic column of water thirty
              meters high and twenty meters wide , about a mile to the left of the boat. As will
              often happen with the phenomenon of the 1978 Adriatic flap, as it was called, the
              existing sources will report different details for each individual case, thus making
              a precise and organic reconstruction of the facts particularly difficult.

Description   Two fishermen who were on the coast at 6.45pm observed something moving
of sighting   rapidly through the water. The object had the shape of a rotating hemispherical
              dome and emanated a pulsating yellow light from inside. At the base it had a dark
              band and in the upper part of the dome it had a small, squat cylinder; a rectangular
              panel was located on the lower dark band. Its speed was fast and it traveled about
              100 meters every five seconds and emitted a strange hum. The object, having
              reached about fifty meters from the witnesses, turned in a southerly direction
              towards the cliff and then stopped. Under the surface of the water, witnesses
              noticed a large and compact structure. At the same moment the two witnesses
              raised their eyes to the sky and saw a round, bluish object, pointed at the sides.
73
                 The two witnesses pointed their flashlight at this object and it apparently reversed
                 its direction, pointing towards the two men who, frightened, ran away.

Description of At around 8.00 pm, seven miles from the coast, the owner of the Laura Madre,
sighting       who was skeptical about all the rumors regarding the Adriatic phenomena,
               noticed a large red light on the bow of the fishing boat . Thinking it was a signal
               buoy, he diverted his course, but later realized that it was not a buoy, but rather an
74
                 object on the surface of the water that gave off a light. The object moved
                 continuously, according to the Laura Madre's maneuvers and despite the constant
                 changes of course to avoid it, it was always under the boat. Panicked, the witness
                 reversed course and was followed to the port by the red light, which continued to
                 maneuver around the fishing boat. Finally, due to shock, the witness was rushed
                 to hospital.

Description of The fisherman Antonio Pallesca, on board the "Libera" motor fishing vessel,
sighting       observed an intense red light that remained to the east no more than three or four
               miles from the coast. Some sources speak of a distance from the boat of only two

               exists regarding the events in the Adriatic in 1978. Subsequently, wanting to head

               the ground. Since the sea was rising, the sailor decided to return to port; the two

               sources) who saw a similar phenomenon the previous evening. At the same time
               as the sighting, some radars of other fishing boats in the area malfunctioned and
               gave no readings.

Description of Certainly the most famous case of the entire Adriatic wave of 1978, although in
sighting       truth there are much more interesting sightings. This case achieved great
               notoriety only because the crew of the Harbor Master's patrol boat witnessed the
               incident and therefore both journalistic and ufological sources of the time always
               considered the sighting to be reliable and safe. But, it is repeated, that throughout
               the entire Adriatic wave there were undoubtedly more strange and interesting
               cases.
               After this brief but necessary introduction, let's analyze what was observed that
               evening around 8.45 pm. The crew of the patrol boat CP 2018, commanded by
               Lieutenant Vitale Bellomo ( although some sources report the name of Lieutenant
               Michele Rubino), saw a red sphere rising from the water at high speed, with an
               inclination of about 45°; the observation lasted only four or five seconds. The
               light remained still in the sky at an altitude of about 300-400 meters and then
               went out, leaving a bluish trail in the sky. The on-board radar reported nothing
80
              and in that stretch the tide was just 23 meters deep. Only about two hours after the
              sighting there were magnetic anomalies on the on-board radar, but not during the
              observation of the light.

Description   It was 07:10 on 14 December 1978 and, like every morning, the photographer
of sighting   Sgattoni, from San Benedetto del Tronto, had gotten up early. After meeting some

              thirty-six exposures of the roll of film. The object continued its upward journey

              "usological" panorama. First of all because I don't think there is a documented
              case in the world with such a high number of photographs, taken among other
              things by a professional photographer. Secondly because we are finally in the
              presence of a serious investigation carried out by two ufologists with scientific
              investigation methodology.

Description   Certainly one of the most interesting cases in all of UFO phenomenology and in
91
of sighting   particular that relating to USOs is the one that occurred off the coast of Gorgona
              on 22 June 1979.
              During a beautiful summer day, in the afternoon, the Rainbow II yacht (31 meters
              long and 51 tons) was heading towards Corsica, after leaving Viareggio at
              3.45pm. The owner, an industrialist from Milan, was in broth with his wife and
              children, as well as the crew of eleven people. Around 6.30 pm the calm and
              beauty of the day were interrupted by a disconcerting episode to say the least:
              about three miles ahead, they observed an enormous cylindrical object, black in
              colour. The object rose above the surface of the sea, similar to a tower, coming out
              of the water for about 30 meters. At that point the motorboat approached slowly,
              being able to notice its shape and enormous dimensions even more. Once we
              reached about a mile and a half away, the object sank with a great crash and a boil
              of the waters, taking no more than three seconds to disappear. Indirect witnesses
              to the sighting were also the Port Authority of Livorno who remained in
              continuous radio contact with the yacht's crew and in particular with its radio
              operator Guzzi. But the phenomenon did not end with the disappearance of the
              object since the ship, despite the compasses indicating the right course, drifted 12
              miles southwards, finding itself in Bastia (the expected arrival was in
              Macinaggio). The drift proved inexplicable given, among other things, the calm
              sea, the lack of currents and the correct functioning of the compass. Another
              curious detail was given by the dog on board which, during the manifestation of
              the phenomenon, barked and gave clear signs of fear and terror.
              It is not known exactly what happened and in fact, the case is still one of two
              actual uses of the catalogue. The greatest regret is that there was no investigation
              carried out with scientific investigation methodology and all that scholars have are
              numerous, but repetitive, newspaper articles. An attempt to investigate at least the
              witnesses was made by the GORU group of La Spezia, who however had the great
              merit of recovering the messages from the Compamare of Livorno and the
              Maridipar of La Spezia warning boats of the presence of a floating obstacle in that
              stretch of sea.
              It is clear that the case was classified as USO, even if there was no serious
              investigation, with a scientific investigation methodology, because the journalistic
              sources and the investigations present are very detailed, at least for the details of
              the object sighted and the testimony of the witnesses.

Description   A meteorite was seen falling into the sea near the beach, about 150 meters from
of sighting   the coast, around 6.30pm. Immediately after the impact a tourist grabbed it,
              immediately releasing it because the object began to smoke and became
              incandescent. Its effect ended as soon as it was buried under the sand, but when it
              came into contact with the air again, the object reignited and became incandescent
              again. This is a classic phenomenon of the so-called phosphorus fragment. It is
              likely that the object is some type of military device with a phosphorus
              component that burns at very high temperatures. The case, which more properly
              falls under FortCat, was only included because the object was seen falling into the
              sea and was initially mistaken for a meteorite.

Description   Two amateur fishermen, Giuseppe Bruna and Angelo Randagi, while fishing on
of sighting   the beach, saw, at a certain distance from the shore, some lights that seemed to
              emerge from the water, and then fly away. They didn't pay much attention to the
              sighting, but after just five minutes, about twenty meters from the shore, they saw
              three bright and aligned portholes emerging from the water. The two side lights
              detached themselves from the formation and headed towards the coast and at this
              point Mr. Bruna took a torch and pointed it towards the sea. The beam of light
              illuminated a hairy being about two meters tall, who was standing in water up to
              his knees. The two fishermen immediately felt a sensation of warmth, so much so
              that the wet sand on which they stood barefoot dried quickly and they had the
              sensation of being attracted towards the sea. The two witnesses, at that point, ran
              away in two different directions. Bruna met a Carabinieri patrol and was taken to
              the barracks where he told the story, while Randagi, while running, tripped and
              fell to the ground and saw the lights receding. The vision of the hairy being lasted
              just a few seconds, as the torch light went out, although it is not known whether
              for mysterious reasons or for the fear of the witnesses.

              and began to stare at the witness. The latter, frightened, was hit by a beam of
              blinding colored light coming from the disk. The witness, in a panic, then closed
              the shutters and bolted all the doors and windows of the house.

Description   This case was cataloged as USO because it is the only Italian case present in the
of sighting   UsoCat that was investigated with a scientific ufological investigation
              methodology, by a serious ufologist, who interviewed the witnesses, without any
              prejudice or preconception.
              Three people who were longline fishing on board a small boat, during the night,
              noticed that between 500 and 1,000 meters from them the sea had become clear,
              as if a large wake had become visible in a particular point. One of the three looked
              at the time and it was 03:00. Thinking that it was a surfacing submarine, the three
              slowed down the speed of the boat and at that point they saw an object, probably
              light gray in color and with slightly blurry outlines, which emerged from the sea
              without any noise and without generating splashes. The object emerged at an
              angle of 60°-70° and disappeared into the sky at very high speed. Shortly after
              rising into the sky, it quickly changed direction. The investigator concludes his
              report by corroborating the testimony of the three, who are serious and
              well-known people and who, as stated by the investigator himself, could very well
              have added details if they had wanted to invent the story.
              Personally I consider this case a USE only because a scientific investigation was
              carried out with a serious methodology; the story itself, in the personal opinion of
              the UsoCat curator, could be classified as "insufficient information", as there are
              many within this catalogue.

Description   While he was returning home on his scooter, a student saw a cruise ship docked
of sighting   the apparent size of the islet behind an islet called Rocca San Nicola. Having

109
      entered the house, before going to bed, he took a last look at the ship, which had a
      series of colored lights, mostly white and, in the central part, others red and green.
      Suddenly this supposed ship took flight from the sea, and then suddenly
      disappeared into the sky. Years later the sighting was also confirmed by another
      witness. The event occurred between the 8th and 21st of the month.
      For completeness of the sources, the article that appeared in Ufo Notiziario n. is
      transcribed in its entirety. 48: “ It is an established fact that the sighting of a UFO
      or witnessing a strange phenomenon perhaps happens only once in a lifetime and
      even more difficult is the fact that the subject is able to find witnesses even after
      some time , or witnesses further anomalous phenomena. But for CGP this is not
      the                                                                                 case.
      Let's start with order CGP, from Licata, 34 years old, university student in
      architecture, at 3.30 am on a Saturday in the second or third week of August 1988
      he was returning home on his scooter after helping his parents in the club of their
      property. The route goes from Torre di Gaffe to the house which is located in front
      of a small island near the sea shore (about 200 meters away) called Rocca di S.
      Nicola. As he is approaching home he sees lights behind the islet and thinks that it
      could be a "cruise ship" docked behind the islet and of comparable size to it. He
      returns home and looks out onto the balcony looking at the sea. The sky was clear
      and starry and the lights behind the islet coming from the "cruise ship" are still
      there. In fact, the dimensions of the object located behind the islet are notable and
      a large number of multicolored lights surround it. Most of them white and a
      certain number red and green concentrated in the center of the "ship" where he
      presumed the ship 's main mast was . The curiosity to see who is having fun on the
      "cruise ship " pushes him to take binoculars . But just as he is focusing on the
      object, the "ship" instantly rises above the islet . Startled, he moves the binoculars
      aside, just in time to see the object dart to the left, traveling at great speed along
      the beach to Torre di Gaffe. He starts calling his parents, but they are busy with
      other things... (they are going to sleep) and don't listen to him. The object stops for
      an instant and returns very quickly to the point where it started, that is, above the
      islet, remains suspended for another instant and then shoots away vertically,
      disappearing from sight in just a few seconds. Noises: none! The entire episode
      lasted about 15 minutes in total. After about 8 years he finds himself with his
      brother at a party and meets by chance with a sailor (G.). We discuss this and that
      and the sailor, among the things that happen to him while sailing, says that once a
      strange episode had happened to him, of a luminous object that was stationary and
      then shot away horizontally, then returned to its original place. first and then it
      rose very quickly vertically. Coincidentally, he was in the same area, but by the
      sea, where the first sighting of CGP had taken place. Finally, in 2002, he found
      himself at home in Licata discussing with his cousins. We also discuss UFOs and
      a friend present who has a passion for underwater fishing, says that a few years
      earlier he was near the Rocca di S. Nicola (!) fishing and all of a sudden he sees
      the sea above him light up; he quickly comes out to the surface (fortunately the
      seabed behind the Rock is about 10 meters deep, otherwise there would have been
      trouble!) just in time to see a large disk that rises vertically upwards and
      disappears after a few seconds".

Description   The location of the sighting is Lago del Salto which is the largest artificial lake in
of sighting   the Lazio region, created in 1940 after the construction of a 90 meter high dam.
              The lake, also known by the name of Lago del Borgo San Pietro, from the name of
              the town that was submerged, has a depth of about 90 meters and legends and
              stories have always been known relating to gnomes, elves and UFOs who would
              be seen entering and come out of the logo itself. On December 7, the ufologist

              narrow "oriental" eyes. The witness saw the beings enter the cold waters of the
              lake, where they remained immersed for almost half an hour. The beings
              subsequently climbed back onto the disk, which disappeared into the sky. It seems
              that the witness had also taken some photos, which were later stolen from her
              apartment, before the car accident in which she lost her life.
              Even if there is no direct observation of the object touching the water, this case
              can be considered a USE, since the beings that, on the contrary, have a physical
              interaction with the water, came precisely from the flying saucer.

   Number            Date            Location         Category        Assessment          Sources
                  dd/mm/yy
      141         05/03/2000      Torvaianica –           B             Possible      “Il Messaggero”
                                     Rome                               weather       of 06/03/2000;
                                                                        balloon       RAI TG of Lazio
                                                                                      at 7.30 pm on
                                                                                      05/03/2000;
                                                                                      telematic catalog
                                                                                      of          UFO
                                                                                      sightings       in
                                                                                      Lazio in April
                                                                                      2002; CrashCat
                                                                                      case no. 174;
                                                                                      news published
                                                                                      on             the
                                                                                      "ilfaroonline"
                                                                                      website         on
                                                                                      10/07/2021.
Description of A former firefighter, who was walking along the beach, saw a dark object, of a
sighting       color between gray and black, which, after gliding on the water for a few seconds,
               sank about a mile away from the coast. No findings were made by the extensive
               search operations. This time the rescue operations were very extensive, because
               initially a Piper plane that must have taken off from Livorno was missing. Only in
               the evening did it become known that the plane in question had never taken off.

                 We report here the article written by Stefano Innocenti, CISU member, for the
                 Lazio news site www.ilfaoonline.it : It was a Sunday, March 5, 2000, when the
                 Lazio Rai news broadcast news that caused a sensation for a few days: “Today an
                 unidentified flying object (UFO) was spotted near Fiumicino which, according to
                 various witnesses from the Torvaianica beach, fell into the sea. The alleged shape
                 of the object has been compared to a weather balloon or a hang glider. The
                 Carabinieri, Marina and others have so far searched the entire affected area,
                 without any positive findings." This was the brief news that entered the homes of
                 those who were watching the regional news.
                 The following day, an article in the Messaggero dramatized the news in these
                 terms: "A plane ended up in the sea, false alarm in Torvaianica". “Run, there is an
                 aircraft that crashed into the sea, right in front of the Red Gates, – we read in the
                 article – a mile away from the coast”. Two excited but reliable voices, being
                 retired firefighters, alerted yesterday morning in Torvaianica to a massive
                 deployment of forces to search the sea for a 'something' that would have sunk.
                 However, until darkness fell, neither patrol boats nor helicopters found anything.
                 The alarm went off when, around midday, the two former firefighters called 115
                 announcing what they had just seen from the Torvaianica fire brigade. The diving
                 teams and a helicopter left from Rome.
                 Those vehicles were soon joined by two Coast Guard patrol boats that set off
                 from Fiumicino, a Guardia di Finanza vehicle and in the afternoon also a Police
                 aircraft. On the ground there was also collaboration from the Civil Protection
                 volunteers of the 'Echo' group from Pomezia. The crews carefully scanned the
130
              waters in front of Torvaianica, especially after the rumor had spread that a tourist
              plane, a Piper, expected at the Cormorano di Nettuno airfield at 1pm, had never
              arrived. It would have been an aircraft with two passengers on board, both
              journalists, which took off from Livorno. In the early afternoon, however,
              investigators from the Anzio police station tracked down the presumed missing:
              that plane from Livorno had never taken off. As darkness fell, the decision was
              also made to suspend the searches. However, the belief that that unidentified
              flying object could perhaps be a sounding balloon has eased the tension over the
              possibility of sinking."
              So, we have two very respectable witnesses who see something that is not
              identified fall into the sea; the rescue machine is put into operation, but nothing
              can be found. As an absolutely implausible explanation, the fairly common
              explanation of the weather balloon is put forward. There is a big difference
              between a plane and a sounding balloon, not only in terms of shape, but also in
              terms of the speed with which it collides with the sea surface. A weather balloon,
              even if limp, torn or punctured, is still made of very light material and perhaps
              still contains small pockets of helium which would prevent what remains from
              sinking. This hypothesis should therefore be rejected a priori. Any other vehicle
              apart from a metal plane would still have remained afloat for a certain period of
              time and the two spotters in the lookout station, which is usually a fire tower
              located a few tens of meters above sea level inside parks or pine forests, they
              would have had it under control with their binoculars. According to Rai, however,
              the crashed object would have been similar to a hang glider: nothing could be
              more different!

of sighting      company of a girl, when she drew his attention to something bright a short

                 limited to a radius of approximately 70 cm. (but it wasn't a "truncated light", in

                 to leave immediately, the witness instead hurriedly got out of the car and chased

                 vertically, flying upwards, rising very quickly towards the candle-shaped sky and
                 disappearing from sight. With this maneuver, the witness was also hit by splashes
                 of water raised violently by the upward movement of the mysterious body.
                 Having returned to the car with his terrified friend, noting an apparent thermal
                 trace on the gravel, which had darkened in the area affected by the mysterious
                 presence, before leaving he took a small surface sample of the soil with the
                 cardboard part of a notebook, duly torn, without touching it with hands, and
                 carefully placing it in a plastic bag that he had in the vehicle for professional
                 reasons (he is an ARPAT employee). A strange smell had spread in the air, quite
                 similar to the typical one recorded during metal welding operations. The
                 following day, the car's windshield showed greenish reflections in sunlight (the
                 same color as the light emitted by the object) which it was not easy to eliminate
                 when washing the car. The samples (delivered in a plastic bag to Roberto Pinotti)
                 show small gravel pebbles, apparently darkened by the action of the mysterious
                 body, mixed with minute glassy fragments. According to the witness, he would
                 have detached one from a pebble with chrome-vanadium pliers, chipping it and
                 even generating some friction sparks (like those of a flint). Examined by a
                 jeweler in Florence on Pinotti's initiative, his superficial impression (excluding,
                 according to him, that they could be diamonds) is that the transparent fragments
                 are of a vitreous nature.
